BMY — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • It sells to three wholesalers who take title and resell within days, and the price it actually collects is set afterwards by rebate contracts with pharmacy benefit managers, by statutory formulas in Medicare and Medicaid, and since January 2026 by an administered price that the Department of Health and Human Services sets outright for selected products.
  • Three of the six largest revenue lines are now on government-set U.S. prices with staggered start dates: Eliquis from 1 January 2026, Pomalyst from 1 January 2027, and Orencia selected in January 2026 for prices from 2028.
  • Apixaban tablet cost is 2–3% of BMS's net revenue per unit and drives nothing. The entire cost structure of this franchise is the 48.3% paid to Pfizer plus BMS's own undisclosed selling cost.
  • Variable contribution margin is roughly 95–98% and reported margin sits in the high eighties to mid nineties, trending down as the allocation base shrinks.
  • Net price per 30-day equivalent therefore rose roughly 18–23% in the first quarter of government price-setting while list price declined 43%
  • Both the Medicaid unit rebate amount and the IRA inflation rebate are computed as current AMP less a base-date AMP escalated by CPI-U. Cutting AMP reduces that penalty dollar for dollar, and since 1 January 2024 the 100%-of-AMP cap no longer limits the Medicaid rebate.
capital-allocation
  • Nine years and $31.2bn of repurchases went in at an average $63.09 against a $64.72 close.
  • BMS has never stated a hurdle rate, target return or payback threshold for business development anywhere in ten years of transcripts or filings — $72.9bn of cash deployed on external innovation over a decade with no required return published.
capitalization
  • BMS funds a $44.3 billion principal debt stack against roughly $14 billion of annual operating cash flow, with almost no near-term maturities, no financial covenants anywhere in the structure, and a principal-weighted coupon of 4.09%.
  • The $18.3bn plus $21.5bn of milestones is $39.8bn, roughly 90% of the funded debt stack, and none of it is on the balance sheet.
competitive-advantage
  • Bristol Myers Squibb earns excess returns today and will keep earning them for about three more years, and it does not hold a durable competitive advantage. What it holds is a set of granted patents with disclosed expiry dates, and 65.0% of FY2025 revenue sits in products whose exclusivity ends between November 2026 and December 2028.
  • Minimum efficient scale in discovery is high but not prohibitive, and the evidence is that BMS's own newest molecules were bought from Karuna, Mirati, MyoKardia, Celgene, BioNTech and SystImmune, companies with a fraction of its R&D budget. If scale in discovery conferred an advantage, the pipeline would be internally sourced.
  • Only Opdivo and Opdivo Qvantig, $10,287M or 38.9% of the Growth Portfolio, have a disclosed U.S. 2028 date. Orencia and Yervoy have no disclosed U.S. date at all, which is not the same thing as protection: Yervoy's U.S. entry is already marked as passed and Orencia's biosimilar entry is now contractually opened.
  • ROIC was 23.6% in 2017 and 39.9% in 2018 on $10–14bn of invested capital. Post-Celgene it has earned 9.0% to 15.5% on $52–82bn. The $80,269M of total consideration transferred for Celgene more than doubled revenue and cut the return on capital by roughly two thirds.
  • NOPAT was $8,169M in FY2021 and $8,486M in FY2025, up 3.9% over four years, against $31,220M of cash paid for acquisitions in FY2022–FY2025 and $18,007M of acquired IPRD expensed in FY2023–FY2025. Roughly $49bn of gross deployment bought a 3.9% increase in NOPAT.
  • The products driving Growth Portfolio expansion — Reblozyl at a 20–24% royalty to Merck, Breyanzi as autologous CAR-T, Opdivo and Yervoy paying Ono — have structurally worse unit economics than the products they are replacing, which were wholly owned oral small molecules. Mix shift into the Growth Portfolio is dilutive to gross margin, which is the opposite of what a strengthening advantage looks like.
  • And BMS keeps roughly half: over 2016–2025 it booked $94,283M of Eliquis revenue and paid Pfizer $44,963M, 47.7% of every revenue dollar, a ratio that has never moved outside 47.0–48.3% in ten years and has moved against BMS.
competitive-landscape
  • Inside the Growth Portfolio, BMS is the scale leader in exactly one business line, and it is materially sub-scale in most of the others. Breyanzi's $484M in Q2 2026 exceeded Gilead's entire cell-therapy franchise of $417M for the first time. In checkpoint inhibitors it is a durable but shrinking number two, at 20.8% of the four-player pool with clean USD filing disclosure against Merck's 64.0%, down from 23.0% in FY2023.
  • The competitor closing fastest is AstraZeneca, whose Imfinzi compounded at 19.6% a year over FY2023–FY2025 and grew 31% in H1 2026 against the Opdivo family's 6.9% two-year rate.
  • The structural threat is a class change rather than a share change: if PD-(L)1 × VEGF bispecifics displace PD-1 monotherapy in first-line NSCLC, both the $10,287M nivolumab franchise and Merck's $31,680M re-base.
  • In CD19 lymphoma BMS is winning outright: Breyanzi went from 27.5% to 42.5% of the three-product CD19 pool in one year, on a label covering LBCL, FL, CLL/SLL, MCL and MZL against Yescarta's narrower one. In BCMA myeloma it is losing outright: Abecma's share of the two-player market declined from 29.7% to 18.5% in a single year
  • Sotyktu was approved in September 2022 with a differentiated mechanism and more than three years later is 12.8% the size of Otezla, the PDE4 inhibitor it was meant to displace, while Otezla itself has turned down, 21% lower year over year in Q2 2026. Over the same window AbbVie took Skyrizi from $7,763M to $17,562M. BMS's entire immunology line is 17.7% of AbbVie's two lead immunology brands.
  • Here BMS is the scale leader by a wide and still-widening margin: about 79.5% of U.S. branded direct-oral-anticoagulant revenue and roughly two-thirds of the branded market globally.
  • Medicare lenalidomide claims grew from 361,641 in 2022 to 468,173 in 2025, and Q1 2026 annualises to 474,944; dasatinib claims grew from 44,274 in 2024 to 64,333 in 2025; nab-paclitaxel Part B claims rose 15% in 2025. Every dollar BMS lost went to a competitor at a lower price, not to a shrinking patient population, so there is no volume recovery to model.
critical-points
  • Against that, the products with a disclosed U.S. exclusivity date beyond 2030 total $7,593M, 15.8% of revenue. AbbVie escaped the same shape with two brands at $6,753M and $2,824M compounding above 39% a year; BMS has to make almost everything work.
  • Eliquis is 30% of revenue and about 21% of gross profit, so both the risk and the 2026 upside are roughly a third smaller than the revenue line implies.
  • Milvexian is an equal profit share with Johnson & Johnson, pumitamig an equal global profit share with BioNTech, and iza-bren a U.S. profit share with SystImmune whose split is not disclosed. Reblozyl already pays Merck 22.6% of net sales, and the Opdivo family pays Ono 4% in North America and 15% elsewhere. Reported gross margin will rise as Eliquis exits — mechanically, because half of Eliquis's cost leaves with its revenue — and then decline again as the partnered assets arrive.
expectations
  • Management and the street agree almost exactly on 2026 and 2027 and have no published disagreement at all, because consensus is anchored on a guidance range issued eight days before these estimates were collected.
  • The consensus 2027 revenue line implies the Growth Portfolio decelerating from roughly +11% to about +5% with nothing inside it losing exclusivity, which either reflects a flat-to-down Opdivo franchise offset by eleven compounding brands, or a line built by taking 2026 up and holding the 2027 decline to the size of the guided Eliquis step-down.
  • Revenue and other income are guided low and beaten; operating expense is guided low and overspent, every year, in every direction of revision; gross margin and the tax rate land close.
financial-history
  • The first is that alliance profit sharing runs through cost of products sold — $6,980M to Pfizer and $7,181M to all partners in FY2025 against $14,443M of Eliquis revenue — so Eliquis's rising share of mix drags consolidated gross margin down. The second is intangible impairment charges booked to cost of products sold, $1,839M in 2024 falling to $564M in 2025.
  • Revlimid alone has shed $7,027M of annual revenue since 2022, more than the Growth Portfolio's four largest launches — Reblozyl, Breyanzi, Camzyos and Opdualag — have added in the same span ($4,763M combined).
forward-model
  • The 2026 peak comes from price: roughly $3.1bn of Eliquis growth against a $2.1bn net revenue increase, and Eliquis converts to gross profit at about 49 cents on the dollar.
  • The second runs 2031–2036: Reblozyl 2031, Breyanzi, Sotyktu and Zeposia 2033, Cobenfy 2033 if the pending patent-term restoration grants, Opdualag 2034, Camzyos 2036. Those seven lines are $6,961M of FY2025 revenue and $15,940M of the model's 2030 revenue, 45.6% of that year.
  • Non-GAAP operating margin declines only 5.5 points from 38.1% (2025) to 32.6% (2030) against a 27% revenue decline, because 50.9 cents of every Eliquis dollar leaves through cost of products sold. A dollar of lost Eliquis revenue costs about $0.49 of gross profit; a dollar of lost Opdivo revenue costs about $0.81.
  • With no buybacks, 2% dividend growth and $2bn a year of business development, net debt bottoms at $24.3bn in 2028 and re-levers to $35.3bn and 3.8x EBITDA by 2035, funded by paying an uncovered dividend.
history-inflections
  • In 2016 Opdivo was 2.7x Keytruda's size; by 2025 Keytruda was 3.1x the whole Opdivo franchise. In a two-drug oncology class the first credible first-line label is close to permanent.
  • It responds to scientific setbacks with acquisitions, two to three years after the shock: CheckMate-026 in 2016 was answered by Celgene in 2019, and the Revlimid collapse of 2021–2023 by $23bn in ten weeks in Q1 2024.
  • It does not miss a target publicly; it re-bases the target or retires the metric.
m-and-a
  • Bristol Myers Squibb is a serial acquirer that has bought revenue scale successfully and bought future earnings power badly. Celgene more than doubled revenue from $22,561M (2018) to $48,194M (2025) while basic weighted-average shares rose 24.5%, so it reached the per-share line. Everything bought since has not.
  • BMS has never recorded a goodwill impairment because it routes most deal consideration around goodwill: $32,539M of acquired IPRD was expensed straight to the income statement between 2019 and 2025, of which $23,522M was MyoKardia and Karuna alone, so there is no carrying value left to test.
management-claims
  • Two-thirds of that portfolio is not protected past the cliff. Opdivo, Opdivo Qvantig, Orencia and Yervoy total $16,892M, 64.0% of the Growth Portfolio and 35.0% of company revenue; of those, Opdivo and Qvantig ($10,287M) have a disclosed U.S. 2028 date and Orencia and Yervoy have no disclosed U.S. date at all, with Orencia's biosimilar entry now contractually opened and its price government-set from 2028.
  • BMS's share of the Opdivo-plus-Keytruda revenue pool declined from 28.3% in 2022 to 24.5% in 2025. Keytruda added $6,669M of revenue over FY2023–FY2025 against the Opdivo family's $1,278M, a 5.2x difference in dollar growth from a 2.8x larger base.
  • Consolidated gross margin declines in 2026 because Eliquis, on which BMS earns roughly half of what its revenue line suggests, is guided up 20–25% while Revlimid and Pomalyst at roughly 90–96% gross margin collapse. The constructed Growth Portfolio gross margin is 80–84%. The mechanism reverses after 2028: when Eliquis exits, reported gross margin rises several hundred basis points at the same time as roughly $7.5bn of BMS gross profit disappears.
  • Revlimid FY2024 was guided at $4.0–4.5bn and delivered $5,773M; FY2025 was guided at $2.0–2.5bn and delivered $2,951M; the Legacy Portfolio was guided at −18% to −20% for FY2025 and delivered −15.4%; and FY2026 was guided at −12% to −16% in February and revised to −4% to −6% in July, a swing worth $1.7–2.2bn.
  • The aggregate targets were hit and every composition target was missed.
management-incentives
  • A deal's cost never touches the annual bonus or 25% of the performance-share unit; its revenue lands in a metric worth 35% of the bonus and 40% of the unit. There is no ROIC, cash-flow, leverage or per-share metric anywhere in the plan.
risks
  • BMS's own base rate for what follows: Revlimid peaked at $12,821M in 2021, was $2,951M in FY2025 and is running at about $1.7bn annualised, −87% from peak; Pomalyst went from $3,545M (FY2024) to $816M annualised within one quarter of U.S. generic entry.
  • The bad case is not a surprise; it is the calendar running as disclosed while the two replacement assets fail to arrive.
secular-trends
  • Over 2020–2025 the Growth Portfolio grew from roughly $12,623M to $26,409M, a 15.9% compound rate adding $13,786M, while Legacy ex-Eliquis declined from $20,780M to $7,342M, subtracting $13,438M. The two almost exactly cancelled. Total revenue rose $5,676M, a 2.5% compound rate, and $5,275M of that — 93% — was Eliquis. Excluding Eliquis, revenue was $33,350M in 2020 and $33,751M in 2025: 1.2% cumulative over five years, 0.24% a year.
  • AbbVie absorbed a larger single-product loss over a shorter window and still compounded at 6.0%: Humira went from $14,404M to $4,540M globally over 2023–2025, a $9,864M loss, while Skyrizi plus Rinvoq went from $11,732M to $25,866M, a 143% replacement ratio. BMS's own two-year ratio is 114% and its five-year ratio is 103%, which left Eliquis as the sole source of net growth.
  • It is patent expiry on Revlimid, Pomalyst, Sprycel and Abraxane, not competitive displacement in the products BMS still sells. Within checkpoint inhibitors it lost about two points of class share over two years and regained ground in 2025. Within multiple myeloma the loss on the lenalidomide and pomalidomide molecules is total and permanent. BMS's oncology franchise is being reconstituted at roughly 60% of its former share of a market that grew about 57%.
  • 2026 is a local peak created by the Eliquis list-price cut rather than by demand.
technology
  • Bristol Myers Squibb does not run on a proprietary technology stack in the way that a semiconductor or software franchise does. It runs on granted patents, regulatory exclusivity and a large payer-facing commercial organisation, into which modalities are bought.
  • On any reasonable attribution, internally discovered technology outside Eliquis is under 10% of revenue.
  • BMS's answer is to rent one: pumitamig, licensed from BioNTech in June 2025 for $1.5bn upfront, $2.0bn of non-contingent anniversary payments across 2026–2028, up to $7.6bn of milestones and an equal global profit share. The incumbent with roughly a fifth of class revenue is paying $3.5bn of guaranteed cash for a partner's next-generation molecule.
  • The 2023 R&D Day set three productivity targets — roughly ten INDs a year, a first-in-human-to-approval success rate of about 20%, and a median 6.5 years from first-in-human to approval. No subsequent deck or call in the archive reports against any of them.
value-chain
  • Of the $53 that leaves BMS's U.S. list price, roughly $25 goes to government programmes, $19 to contracted purchasers through chargebacks, and $8 to commercial payers and PBMs. The three wholesalers that physically move 87–90% of the product keep about $1 of operating profit, the smallest slice of any named layer. The profit pool is at BMS and at the contracted purchasers.
  • Two things constrain the business instead: patent expiry limits the volume the company is allowed to sell, and statute sets the price on the largest lines. Eliquis, Pomalyst and Orencia were $20,881M, 43% of FY2025 revenue, and all three are now on or scheduled for administratively set U.S. prices.
  • The gross-to-net reserve was $10,565M at 31 December 2025 on $88,085M of gross product sales, roughly 94 days of gross-to-net payments. Cutting the list price destroys the float: accrued rebates and discounts went from $8,844M at 31 December 2025 to $5,654M at 30 June 2026, with a matching $3,135M cash outflow in the half.

It sells to three wholesalers who take title and resell within days, and the price it actually collects is set afterwards by rebate contracts with pharmacy benefit managers, by statutory formulas in Medicare and Medicaid, and since January 2026 by an administered price that the Department of Health and Human Services sets outright for selected products.

business-explainer · business-model · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callqualifies
As you know and as a reminder that this is a heavy Medicare, Medicaid population, and so we have virtually 100% access across both.
— Adam Lenkowsky, EVP & Chief Commercialization Officer, BMY Q3 2025 earnings call, 2025-10-30
filingsupports ✦ best
Wholesalers are initially invoiced at contractual list prices. Payment terms are typically 30 to 90 days based on customary practices in each country. Revenue is reduced from wholesaler list price at the time of recognition for expected charge-backs, discounts, rebates, sales allowances and product returns ("GTN adjustments"). In the U.S., these GTN adjustments are attributed to various commercial arrangements, managed healthcare organizations and government programs such as Medicare, Medicaid and the 340B program containing various pricing implications, such as mandatory discounts or pricing protection below wholesaler list price.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
Our U.S. business participates in programs with government entities, the most significant of which are the U.S. Department of Defense and the U.S. Department of Veterans Affairs, and other parties, including covered entities under the 340B program, whereby pricing on products is extended below wholesaler list price to participating entities. These entities purchase products through wholesalers at the lower program price and the wholesalers then charge us the difference between their acquisition cost and the lower program price. Accounts receivable is reduced for the estimated amount of unprocessed charge-back claims attributable to a sale (typically within a two to four week time lag).
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
Reductions/(increases) to provisions for product sales made in prior periods resulting from changes in estimates were $485 million for 2025 and $159 million for 2024. The reductions to provisions in 2025 primarily related to lower than expected Medicaid utilization, and the reductions to provisions in 2024 primarily related to the non-U.S. revisions in clawback amounts driven by VAT recoverable estimates. GTN adjustments are primarily a function of product sales volume, regional and payer channel mix, contractual or legislative discounts and rebates. U.S. GTN adjustments percentage increased primarily due to the redesign of the U.S. Medicare Part D program and higher government channel mix, which has higher GTN adjustment percentages.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06

filingsupports
Net product sales represent more than 95 % of total revenues for all periods presented. Products are sold principally to wholesalers, distributors, specialty pharmacies, and to a lesser extent, directly to retailers, hospitals, clinics, government agencies and patients. Customer orders are generally fulfilled within a few days of receipt resulting in minimal order backlog. Contractual performance obligations are usually limited to transfer of control of the product to the customer. The transfer occurs either upon shipment, upon receipt of the product after considering when the customer obtains legal title to the product, or upon infusion for cell therapies and when BMS obtains a right of payment.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
McKesson Corporation 36 % 34 % 33 % Cencora, Inc. 29 % 29 % 29 % Cardinal Health, Inc. 22 % 22 % 23 %
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
Non-U.S. receivables sold on a nonrecourse basis were $ 360 million in 2025, $ 477 million in 2024 and $ 1.0 billion in 2023. Receivables from the three largest customers in the U.S. represented 75 % and 74 % of total trade receivables at December 31, 2025 and 2024, respectively.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
In addition, in the U.S., most of our products are distributed through wholesalers, and if one of these wholesalers should encounter financial or other difficulties, we might be unable to timely collect the amounts that the wholesaler owes us, which could negatively impact our results of operations. We expect that consolidation and integration of pharmacy chains, wholesalers and pharmacy benefit managers will increase competitive and pricing pressures on pharmaceutical manufacturers, including us.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
We participate in the Medicaid Drug Rebate Program ("MDRP"), under which we must pay rebates to state Medicaid programs for our covered outpatient drugs provided to Medicaid beneficiaries, with rebates based on pricing data we report regularly to the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS). We also participate in the Health Resources and Services Administration's 340B program, under which we must offer covered outpatient drugs to statutorily defined covered entities at no more than the 340B program "ceiling price", with that price calculated based on MDRP-reported data.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
In the U.S., we generally determine our months on hand estimates using inventory levels of product on hand and the amount of out-movement provided by our three largest wholesalers, which accounted for approximately 90% of total gross sales of U.S. products during the six months ended June 30, 2026.
— BMY Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, 2026-07-30
filingqualifies
GTN adjustments are primarily a function of product sales volume, regional and payer channel mix, contractual or legislative discounts and rebates.
— BMY Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, 2026-07-30

Medicare reimburses Part B drugs based on average sales price plus a certain percentage to account for physician administration costs, which have been reduced in the hospital outpatient setting. Medicare enters into contracts with private plans to negotiate prices for most patient-administered medicine delivered under Part D.
— AbbVie FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-13

— Pfizer Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, 2026-08-04

filingsupports ✦ best
In August 2024, as part of the first round of government price setting pursuant to the IRA, the HHS announced the "maximum fair price" for a 30-day equivalent supply of Eliquis, which applies to the U.S. Medicare channel effective January 1, 2026. In November 2025, the HHS announced the "maximum fair price" for a 30-day supply of Pomalyst, which applies to the U.S. Medicare channel effective January 1, 2027. In January 2026, the HHS selected Orencia as a medicine subject to "negotiation" for government-set prices beginning in 2028.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
Under the IRA, the HHS can effectively set prices for units of certain single-source drugs and biologics reimbursed under Medicare Part B, Medicare Advantage and Part D. Generally, these government prices apply nine years (for small molecule drugs) or 13 years (for biologics) following FDA approval and will be capped at a statutory ceiling price that is likely to represent a significant discount from average prices to wholesalers and direct purchasers.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06
filingsupports
The IRA directs (i) the federal government to "negotiate" prices for select high-cost Medicare Part D (beginning in 2026) and Part B (beginning in 2028) drugs that are more than nine years (for small-molecule drugs) or 13 years (for biologics) from their initial FDA approval, (ii) manufacturers to pay a rebate for Medicare Part B and Part D drugs when prices increase faster than inflation and (iii) the formation of the Part D Manufacturer Program which replaced the Part D CGDP and established a $2,000 cap for out-of-pocket costs for Medicare beneficiaries as of January 2025, with manufacturers being responsible for 10% of costs up to the $2,000 cap and 20% after that cap is reached.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06

Additionally, in January 2026, the HHS selected Orencia as a medicine subject to "negotiation" for government-set prices beginning in 2028.

It is possible that more of our products could be selected in future years based upon the selection criteria currently utilized by the HHS or potentially expanded future criteria, or that the "maximum fair price" for our previously selected products could be renegotiated, each of which could, among other things, accelerate revenue erosion prior to expiry of intellectual property protections.
— BMY Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, 2026-07-30
filingsupports
On June 16, 2023, BMS filed a lawsuit against HHS and the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services, et al. , challenging the constitutionality of the drug-pricing program in the IRA. That program requires pharmaceutical companies, like BMS, under the threat of significant penalties, to sell certain of their medicines at government-dictated prices. In April 2024, the court denied BMS's motion for summary judgment and granted the government's cross-motion for summary judgment. BMS appealed to the United States Court of Appeals for the Third Circuit. In September 2025, the Third Circuit affirmed the lower court's decision. In December 2025, BMS filed a petition for certiorari at the Supreme Court of the United States, seeking review of the Third Circuit's decision. That petition was denied in May 2026.
— BMY Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, 2026-07-30
filingqualifies
In December 2025, we announced the U.S. Government Agreement pursuant to which BMS will receive certain U.S. tariff relief until January 2029, including for recently imposed tariffs relating to patented pharmaceuticals and associated pharmaceutical ingredients pursuant to the Presidential Proclamation dated April 2, 2026, and will not be subject to future pricing mandates in the U.S. while the agreement remains in effect. However, such exemptions may be terminated or may not be extended.
— BMY Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, 2026-07-30

We participate in the Medicaid Drug Rebate Program ("MDRP"), under which we must pay rebates to state Medicaid programs for our covered outpatient drugs provided to Medicaid beneficiaries, with rebates based on pricing data we report regularly to the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS). We also participate in the Health Resources and Services Administration's 340B program, under which we must offer covered outpatient drugs to statutorily defined covered entities at no more than the 340B program "ceiling price", with that price calculated based on MDRP-reported data.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-06

filingsupports ✦ best
Another competitive challenge we face is from generic pharmaceutical manufacturers. In certain countries, including the U.S. and in the EU, the regulatory approval process exempts generics from costly and time-consuming clinical studies to demonstrate their safety and efficacy, allowing generic manufacturers to rely on the safety and efficacy of the innovator product. As a result, generic pharmaceutical manufacturers typically invest far less in R&D than research-based pharmaceutical companies and therefore can price their products significantly lower than branded products. Accordingly, when a branded product loses its market exclusivity, it normally faces intense price competition from generic forms of the product. Upon the expiration or loss of market exclusivity on a product, we can lose the major portion of that product's revenue in a very short period of time.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb Company Form 10-K, FY2025 — Item 1 Business, "Competition", 2026-02-11
filingsupports
Estimated Minimum Market Exclusivity Date U.S. EU (q) Japan … Eliquis (apixaban) (e) 2028 ^^ 2026 … Opdivo (nivolumab) 2028 2030 2031 Opdivo Qvantig (nivolumab and hyaluronidase-nvhy) (g) ^^ ^^ ++ … Orencia (abatacept) (i) ^^ ^^ ^^ … Yervoy (ipilimumab) (o) ^^ 2026 ^^
— Bristol-Myers Squibb Company Form 10-K, FY2025 — "Estimated Minimum Market Exclusivity Date" table, 2026-02-11
filingqualifies
After the expiration of exclusivity, the rate of revenue decline of a product varies by country. In general, the decline in the U.S. market is more rapid than in most other developed countries, though we have observed rapid declines in a number of EU countries as well. Also, the declines in developed countries tend to be more rapid than in developing countries. The rate of revenue decline after the expiration of exclusivity has also historically been influenced by product characteristics. For example, drugs that are used in a large patient population (e.g., those prescribed by key primary care physicians) tend to experience more rapid declines than drugs in specialized areas of medicine (e.g., oncology). Drugs that are more complex to manufacture (e.g., sterile injectable products) usually experience a slower decline than those that are simpler to manufacture.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb Company Form 10-K, FY2025 — Item 1 Business, "Competition", 2026-02-11

The products driving Growth Portfolio expansion — Reblozyl at a 20–24% royalty to Merck, Breyanzi as autologous CAR-T, Opdivo and Yervoy paying Ono — have structurally worse unit economics than the products they are replacing, which were wholly owned oral small molecules. Mix shift into the Growth Portfolio is dilutive to gross margin, which is the opposite of what a strengthening advantage looks like.

filingsupports
In the pharmaceutical and biotechnology industries, the majority of an innovative product's commercial value is realized during its market exclusivity period. In the U.S. and in some other countries, when market exclusivity expires and generic versions are approved and marketed or when biosimilars are introduced (even if only for a competing product), there are usually very substantial and rapid declines in a product's revenues.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors - 'We could lose market exclusivity of a product earlier than expected', 2026-02-05
filingsupports
As is common in the pharmaceutical industry, BMS expects that sales of its branded products like Orencia, Eliquis and Opdivo will decline after the loss of market exclusivity for such products. Consequently, our future success is highly dependent on our pipeline of new products. There is a high rate of failure inherent in the research and development process for new drugs. As a result, there is a high risk that our investments in research programs will not generate financial returns.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors - pipeline dependence, 2026-02-05
filingsupports

filingqualifies
After the expiration of exclusivity, the rate of revenue decline of a product varies by country. In general, the decline in the U.S. market is more rapid than in most other developed countries, though we have observed rapid declines in a number of EU countries as well. ... The rate of revenue decline after the expiration of exclusivity has also historically been influenced by product characteristics. For example, drugs that are used in a large patient population (e.g., those prescribed by key primary care physicians) tend to experience more rapid declines than drugs in specialized areas of medicine (e.g., oncology). Drugs that are more complex to manufacture (e.g., sterile injectable products) usually experience a slower decline than those that are simpler to manufacture.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business - post-exclusivity erosion shape, 2026-02-05

Milvexian is an equal profit share with Johnson & Johnson, pumitamig an equal global profit share with BioNTech, and iza-bren a U.S. profit share with SystImmune whose split is not disclosed. Reblozyl already pays Merck 22.6% of net sales, and the Opdivo family pays Ono 4% in North America and 15% elsewhere. Reported gross margin will rise as Eliquis exits — mechanically, because half of Eliquis's cost leaves with its revenue — and then decline again as the partnered assets arrive.

We typically do not retain any rights to another party's product or intellectual property after an alliance terminates. The loss of rights to one or more products that are marketed and sold by us pursuant to an alliance could be material to our results of operations ... Alliance agreements may be structured to terminate on specific dates, upon the product's patent expiration date or without an expiry date. Profit sharing payments typically have no expiration date while royalty payments typically cease upon loss of market exclusivity, including patent expiration.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors - alliance dependence, 2026-02-05

Now the broader pricing dynamics starting this year for Eliquis was the impetus for us to reevaluate our pricing strategy. And of course, there's some pushes and pulls. Recall the IRA price was effectuated January 1, and this includes the removal of the Medicare Part D liability, both in the initiation and in the catastrophic phase. We also finalized our $0 Medicaid agreement with the administration. And we took a step back and were able to reassess our commercial contracting strategy as well. The roughly 40% WAC reduction eliminates the inflationary penalties or CPI penalties of statutory rebates that have been accumulating over many years for the brand. And taken together, the continued increase in Eliquis market share in the United States, coupled with these net pricing changes, they're going to enable Eliquis to be an important driver of growth this year.

filingsupports
We assess the goodwill balance within our single reporting unit annually and whenever events or changes in circumstances indicate the carrying value of goodwill may not be recoverable to determine whether any impairment in this asset may exist and, if so, the extent of such impairment. Goodwill is reviewed for impairment by assessing qualitative factors, including comparing our market capitalization to the carrying value of our assets.
— BMY FY2019 Form 10-K, MD&A Critical Accounting Policies — Goodwill, 2019-12-31
filingqualifies
Goodwill represents the going-concern value associated with future product discovery beyond the existing pipeline and expected value of synergies resulting from cost savings and avoidance not attributed to identifiable assets. Goodwill is not deductible for tax purposes.

We make certain judgments to determine whether transactions should be accounted for as acquisitions of assets or as business combinations. If it is determined that substantially all of the fair value of gross assets acquired in a transaction is concentrated in a single asset (or a group of similar assets), the transaction is treated as an acquisition of assets. We evaluate the inputs, processes, and outputs associated with the acquired set of activities and assets.
— BMY FY2024 Form 10-K, MD&A Critical Accounting Estimates — Acquisition and Intangible Assets Valuations, 2024-12-31

filingsupports
If the assets acquired do not meet the definition of a business, primarily because the inputs and processes do not significantly contribute to the ability to create outputs or substantially all of the relative fair value was allocated to a single asset, the transaction is accounted for as an asset acquisition rather than a business combination and no goodwill is recorded. In addition, in an asset acquisition, acquired in-process research and development ("IPRD") assets with no alternative future use are expensed to Acquired IPRD.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 1 Basis of Presentation and Significant Accounting Policies — Acquisitions, 2025-12-31

filingsupports
In transactions accounted for as acquisitions of assets, no goodwill is recorded and contingent consideration, such as payments upon achievement of various developmental, regulatory and commercial milestones, generally is not recognized at the acquisition date. In an asset acquisition, upfront payments allocated to IPRD projects at the acquisition date are expensed unless there is an alternative future use. In addition, product development milestones are expensed upon achievement.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, MD&A Critical Accounting Estimates — Acquisition and Intangible Assets Valuations, 2025-12-31
filingsupports
Goodwill is tested at least annually for impairment by assessing qualitative factors in determining whether it is more likely than not that the fair value of net assets is below their carrying amounts. Examples of qualitative factors assessed include BMS's share price, financial performance compared to budgets, long-term financial plans, macroeconomic, industry and market conditions as well as the substantial excess of fair value over the carrying value of net assets from the annual impairment test performed in a prior year.

Qualified innovative biologics receive 12 years of regulatory exclusivity, meaning that the FDA may not approve a biosimilar version until 12 years after the innovative biologic was first approved by the FDA. Our marketed biologics include Opdivo, Opdivo Qvantig, Orencia, Yervoy, Reblozyl, Breyanzi, Opdualag, and Abecma.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business - Biologics (includes CAR-T cell therapy products), 2026-02-11
filingsupports
In the pharmaceutical and biotechnology industries, the majority of an innovative product's commercial value is realized during its market exclusivity period. In the U.S. and in some other countries, when market exclusivity expires and generic versions are approved and marketed or when biosimilars are introduced (even if only for a competing product), there are usually very substantial and rapid declines in a product's revenues.

— Pfizer FY2025 Form 10-K, patent and exclusivity discussion, 2026-02-26

Consolidated gross margin declines in 2026 because Eliquis, on which BMS earns roughly half of what its revenue line suggests, is guided up 20–25% while Revlimid and Pomalyst at roughly 90–96% gross margin collapse. The constructed Growth Portfolio gross margin is 80–84%. The mechanism reverses after 2028: when Eliquis exits, reported gross margin rises several hundred basis points at the same time as roughly $7.5bn of BMS gross profit disappears.

Performance-Vested Restricted Stock Units (40% of total LTI award) — These awards have the potential to vest at 0% to 200% of target in one-third increments during a three-year performance period and are earned based on AbbVie's return on invested capital (ROIC) relative to a group made up of companies that are constituents in either the S&P Pharmaceutical, Biotech, and Life Science Index or the NYSE Arca Pharmaceutical Index.

filingsupports
ROIC is a non-GAAP measure equal to the quotient of (i) adjusted net earnings, as outlined in Appendix B, divided by (ii) average invested capital. Average invested capital is equal to the average of the following over fiscal years 2024 and 2025: total stockholders' equity, plus long-term debt (which excludes any long-term lease obligations included as long-term debt on the company's balance sheet).
— AbbVie 2026 Proxy Statement (DEF 14A), 2026-03-23

— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, MD&A — results of operations overview, 2026-02-05
filingqualifies
After the expiration of exclusivity, the rate of revenue decline of a product varies by country. In general, the decline in the U.S. market is more rapid than in most other developed countries, though we have observed rapid declines in a number of EU countries as well. Also, the declines in developed countries tend to be more rapid than in developing countries. The rate of revenue decline after the expiration of exclusivity has also historically been influenced by product characteristics. For example, drugs that are used in a large patient population (e.g., those prescribed by key primary care physicians) tend to experience more rapid declines than drugs in specialized areas of medicine (e.g., oncology). Drugs that are more complex to manufacture (e.g., sterile injectable products) usually experience a slower decline than those that are simpler to manufacture.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1. Business — Competition, rate of decline after exclusivity expiration, 2026-02-05
filingsupports
In the pharmaceutical and biotechnology industries, the majority of an innovative product’s commercial value is realized during its market exclusivity period. In the U.S. and in some other countries, when market exclusivity expires and generic versions are approved and marketed or when biosimilars are introduced (even if only for a competing product), there are usually very substantial and rapid declines in a product’s revenues.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A. Risk Factors — 'We could lose market exclusivity of a product earlier than expected', 2026-02-05

Peer: Amgen

filingsupports
Because the FDA approval process permits generic manufacturers to rely on the safety and efficacy data of the innovator product rather than having to conduct their own costly and time-consuming clinical trials, generic manufacturers can often develop and market their competing versions of our small molecule products at much lower prices. For example, following loss of exclusivity of patents directed to cinacalcet, the active ingredient in our small molecule calcimimetic Sensipar, we lost a significant share of the market and corresponding revenues in a very short period of time.
— Amgen Inc. FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors — competition from biosimilars and generics, 2026-02-11
filingsupports
We have experienced adverse effects from biosimilar competition on our originator product sales. Companies have launched versions of EPOGEN, NEUPOGEN, Neulasta and ENBREL (Canada only) with U.S. ENBREL biosimilars approved but not launched. Once multiple biosimilar versions of one of our originator products have launched, competition intensifies rapidly, resulting in accelerated net price declines for both the reference and the biosimilar products. For example, our patents for RANKL antibodies, including sequences, for Prolia and XGEVA expired in February 2025 in the United States and in November 2025 in select countries in Europe, and we expect accelerated sales erosion driven by increased competition, as multiple biosimilars have launched in the United States and ROW.
— Amgen FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A. Risk Factors — competition from biosimilars and generics, 2026-02-11

filingsupports
The Company depends upon patents to provide it with exclusive marketing rights for its products for some period of time. filingsupports
We may experience difficulties or delays in the development and commercialization of new products. Our ability to replace revenue from products that lose patent protection is directly dependent on our ability to successfully develop and commercialize new products in a timely manner. As is common in the pharmaceutical industry, BMS expects that sales of its branded products like Orencia, Eliquis and Opdivo will decline after the loss of market exclusivity for such products. Consequently, our future success is highly dependent on our pipeline of new products. There is a high rate of failure inherent in the research and development process for new drugs. As a result, there is a high risk that our investments in research programs will not generate financial returns. Compounds or products may appear promising in development but fail to reach market within the expected timeframe, or at all. We have experienced setbacks and may continue to do so.

competitive-position · technology · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callsupports

— Adam Lenkowsky, Executive VP & Chief Commercialization Officer, Bristol-Myers Squibb Company, Q2 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-07-30
filingsupports ✦ best
In the pharmaceutical industry, the majority of an innovative product’s commercial value is usually realized during the period in which the product has market exclusivity. A product’s market exclusivity is generally determined by two forms of intellectual property: patent rights held by the innovator company and any regulatory forms of exclusivity to which the innovative drug is entitled.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Products, Intellectual Property and Product Exclusivity, 2026-02-11
filingsupports
Another competitive challenge we face is from generic pharmaceutical manufacturers. In certain countries, including the U.S. and in the EU, the regulatory approval process exempts generics from costly and time-consuming clinical studies to demonstrate their safety and efficacy, allowing generic manufacturers to rely on the safety and efficacy of the innovator product. ... Accordingly, when a branded product loses its market exclusivity, it normally faces intense price competition from generic forms of the product. Upon the expiration or loss of market exclusivity on a product, we can lose the major portion of that product's revenue in a very short period of time.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Competition, 2026-02-11
filingsupports
The growth and consolidation of MCOs and PBMs in the U.S. has also been a major factor in the healthcare marketplace. As MCOs and PBMs have been consolidating into fewer, larger entities, they have also been enhancing their purchasing strength and share of voice within the market. To successfully compete for formulary position with MCOs and PBMs, we must often demonstrate that our products offer not only medical benefits but also cost advantages as compared with other forms of care. Exclusion of a product from a formulary can lead to its sharply reduced usage in patient populations due to higher out-of-pocket costs to patients.
— BMY FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 Business — Pricing, Price Constraints and Market Access, 2026-02-11
filingsupports
The following chart shows our key products together with the year in which the earliest basic exclusivity loss (patent rights or regulatory exclusivity) is currently estimated to occur in the U.S., the EU and Japan (the "estimated minimum market exclusivity date"). ... Generally, the estimated minimum market exclusivity date in the table below pertains to the end of regulatory exclusivity or the COM patent expiration for the respective products and PTR if granted.

Drug candidates can fail at any stage of the process, and even late-stage product candidates sometimes fail to receive regulatory approval. filingsupports
The Company depends upon patents to provide it with exclusive marketing rights for its products for some period of time. Loss of patent protection for one of the Company’s products typically leads to a significant and rapid loss of sales for that product as lower priced generic or biosimilar versions become available. In the case of products that contribute significantly to the Company’s sales, the loss of market exclusivity can have a material adverse effect on the Company’s business, cash flows, results of operations, financial condition and prospects.
— Merck & Co. FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors, 2026-02-24

Of the $53 that leaves BMS's U.S. list price, roughly $25 goes to government programmes, $19 to contracted purchasers through chargebacks, and $8 to commercial payers and PBMs. The three wholesalers that physically move 87–90% of the product keep about $1 of operating profit, the smallest slice of any named layer. The profit pool is at BMS and at the contracted purchasers.

filingsupports
Revenue is reduced from wholesaler list price at the time of recognition for expected charge-backs, discounts, rebates, sales allowances and product returns ("GTN adjustments"). In the U.S., these GTN adjustments are attributed to various commercial arrangements, managed healthcare organizations and government programs such as Medicare, Medicaid and the 340B program containing various pricing implications, such as mandatory discounts or pricing protection below wholesaler list price.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-05
filingsupports
GTN adjustments are primarily a function of product sales volume, regional and payer channel mix, contractual or legislative discounts and rebates. U.S. GTN adjustments percentage increased primarily due to the redesign of the U.S. Medicare Part D program and higher government channel mix, which has higher GTN adjustment percentages.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-05
filingsupports
The growth and consolidation of MCOs and PBMs in the U.S. has also been a major factor in the healthcare marketplace. As MCOs and PBMs have been consolidating into fewer, larger entities, they have also been enhancing their purchasing strength and share of voice within the market. ... Exclusion of a product from a formulary can lead to its sharply reduced usage in patient populations due to higher out-of-pocket costs to patients. ... A lower overall cost of therapy, usually provided as a rebate to the PBM, is also an important factor.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-05
filingqualifies
Charge-backs and cash discounts are reflected as a reduction to receivables and settled through the issuance of credits to the customer, typically within one month. All other GTN adjustments, are reflected as a liability and settled through cash payments to the customer, typically within various time periods ranging from a few months to one year.
— Bristol-Myers Squibb FY2025 Form 10-K, 2026-02-05
